origin	https://git.ardenone.com/jedarden/declarative-config.git (fetch)

apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: WorkflowTemplate
metadata:
  name: bead-rs-ci
  namespace: argo-workflows
  labels:
    app: bead-rs-ci
spec:
  # Backstop against a run that hangs before its pod ever starts (unbound
  # PVC, unschedulable, missing secret). Per-step activeDeadlineSeconds does
  # not cover that case: on 2026-08-07 five needle-ci runs sat Running for
  # 8h against a 3600s step limit. ttlStrategy only reaps COMPLETED runs, so
  # without a workflow-level cap they are never terminated and never GCd.
  # Basis: sum of per-step deadlines x attempts = 3600s, +50% headroom.
  activeDeadlineSeconds: 7200
  entrypoint: ci
  serviceAccountName: argo-workflow
  synchronization:
    mutexes:
      - name: bead-rs-ci-mutex
  templates:
    - name: ci
      activeDeadlineSeconds: 3600
      container:
        image: debian:bookworm
        command: [bash, -c]
        args:
          - |
            set -ex

            # System dependencies. rusqlite is built with the "bundled"
            # feature (vendors and compiles its own SQLite), so no
            # libsqlite3-dev is needed here -- build-essential is enough.
            apt-get update && apt-get install -y \
              git curl build-essential pkg-config libssl-dev

            # Install gh CLI
            curl -fsSL https://cli.github.com/packages/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg | dd of=/usr/share/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg
            echo "deb [arch=$(dpkg --print-architecture) signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg] https://cli.github.com/packages stable main" | tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/github-cli.list > /dev/null
            apt-get update && apt-get install -y gh

            # Install Rust stable
            curl --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 -sSf https://sh.rustup.rs | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain stable
            . "$HOME/.cargo/env"
            rustup component add clippy rustfmt
            rustc --version

            # Clone from trusted hardcoded source (ignore webhook payload)
            git clone --depth 1 "https://git.ardenone.com/jedarden/bead-rs.git" /workspace
            cd /workspace

            COMMIT=$(git rev-parse HEAD)

            # CI checks
            cargo fmt --all -- --check
            cargo check --all-targets
            cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
            cargo test

            echo "CI passed!"

            # Extract version from Cargo.toml
            VERSION=$(grep -m1 '^version' Cargo.toml | sed 's/.*= *"\(.*\)".*/\1/')
            echo "Version: ${VERSION}"

            # Tag-to-Forgejo (draft-revert root-cause fix, 2026-08-16): the
            # GitHub repo is a Forgejo push mirror, and every mirror sync
            # PRUNES refs that exist only on GitHub. GitHub demotes a
            # published release to draft when its tag is deleted, so the tag
            # must exist on Forgejo BEFORE any GitHub release references it.
            # Plain push, never force: a tag already on Forgejo at a
            # different commit fails loudly here.
            git tag "v${VERSION}" 2>/dev/null || [ "$(git rev-parse "refs/tags/v${VERSION}^{commit}")" = "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" ]
            echo "Pushing tag v${VERSION} to Forgejo"
            set +x
            git push "https://x-token:${FORGEJO_TOKEN}@git.ardenone.com/jedarden/bead-rs.git" "refs/tags/v${VERSION}"
            set -x

            # Skip only if the version is genuinely PUBLISHED (not a draft).
            # `gh release create` has been observed leaving/reverting a
            # release to draft in this environment under unclear
            # circumstances (confirmed recurring on v0.1.0 of this repo,
            # 2026-08-12) -- mirrors the same backstop already in place in
            # needle-workflowtemplate.yml.
            IS_DRAFT=$(gh release view "v${VERSION}" --repo jedarden/bead-rs --json isDraft --jq '.isDraft' 2>/dev/null || echo "absent")

            if [ "$IS_DRAFT" = "false" ]; then
              echo "Release v${VERSION} already published — skipping"
              exit 0
            fi

            if [ "$IS_DRAFT" = "true" ]; then
              echo "Release v${VERSION} exists as a DRAFT — publishing it in place"
              gh release edit "v${VERSION}" --repo jedarden/bead-rs --draft=false
              echo "Release v${VERSION} published"
              exit 0
            fi

            # Build the release binary (the installed CLI is `bead`;
            # generate-man-pages is a doc-generation dev tool, not part of
            # the release artifact)
            cargo build --release --bin bead

            # Create GitHub release
            gh release create "v${VERSION}" \
              --repo jedarden/bead-rs \
              --title "bead-rs v${VERSION}" \
              --notes "Release v${VERSION}

            Built from commit: ${COMMIT}

            Clean-room Rust task-coordination CLI for agent fleets. Installed
            binary: \`bead\`. See README.md for install and usage.
            " \
              --target main \
              ./target/release/bead

            echo "Release v${VERSION} created!"
        env:
          - name: GH_TOKEN
            valueFrom:
              secretKeyRef:
                name: github-webhook-secret
                key: token
          - name: FORGEJO_TOKEN
            valueFrom:
              secretKeyRef:
                name: forgejo-webhook-token
                key: token
        resources:
          requests:
            cpu: 1500m
            memory: 3Gi
          limits:
            cpu: 3000m
            memory: 5Gi

# Declarative Configuration Guide

This repository is the source of truth for cluster state. ArgoCD reconciles
every cluster from `main`, so a pushed manifest may deploy immediately.

## Before Editing

- Inspect `git status --short` and preserve all unrelated work. This repository
  is edited concurrently by agents on multiple machines.
- Read the closest nested `AGENTS.md`, especially the target cluster guide.
- Before starting, update with `git pull --rebase origin main` when doing so
  will not disturb another worker's uncommitted changes. Never merge or
  force-push.

## Changes and Delivery

- Make all desired-state changes in this repository. Do not directly mutate
  ArgoCD-managed cluster resources.
- Keep commits narrowly scoped. After each commit, push immediately to
  `origin main`; do not batch local commits.
- If a push is rejected, run `git pull --rebase origin main`, resolve carefully,
  and push again. Do not overwrite another contributor's work.
- Validate manifests before committing. After pushing, use read-only cluster or
  ArgoCD inspection to confirm the relevant Application is `Synced` and
  `Healthy`; a successful push alone is not completion.

## Secrets

- Never commit plaintext credentials or a populated `kind: Secret`. Use the
  repository's documented template and SealedSecret workflow.
- Before committing, ensure both `pre-commit` and `gitleaks` are installed and
  that `pre-commit install` has been run for this clone. Hooks are local and do
  not arrive with a clone.
- Do not bypass failed secret-scanning hooks. If sensitive data appears in any
  output or diff, stop and remove it without repeating the value.

Kubernetes layout, naming, resource, storage, ingress, ArgoCD, and validation
rules are in `k8s/AGENTS.md`.

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-14-  idempotently and their pods are not ArgoCD-owned, so they are never pruned
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-15-  and hold resource reservations forever. Use a **Deployment with an internal
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-16-  scheduling loop**; for genuinely one-shot work use an Argo WorkflowTemplate.

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-120-- This is a dedicated Hetzner server connected exclusively via Tailscale
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:121:- kubectl access to clusters is via credential-free kubectl-proxy over Tailscale (no kubeconfigs with tokens on disk)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-122-- GitOps via ArgoCD — **all cluster changes go through `jedarden/declarative-config`** (k8s/ path); this includes not just `kubectl apply`/`create` but ALSO any live mutation of a managed resource: `kubectl scale`, `delete`, `patch`, `edit`, `annotate`, `rollout restart`, deleting ScaledObjects, etc. — even for one-off triage, emergencies, or "temporary" fixes. ArgoCD `selfHeal` reverts live edits anyway, so they don't stick; worse, they fight the controller. The ONLY sanctioned way to change desired state is: edit the manifest in declarative-config → commit → push → let ArgoCD sync (force the sync via the ArgoCD Application if it's lagging — that *applies* the repo, it doesn't bypass it). The sole exception is genuinely orphaned objects ArgoCD does not own and will never recreate (e.g. zombie pods beyond a ReplicaSet's desired count) — deleting those is cleanup, not a config change.
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-123-- **Rackspace Spot storage:** always use `sata` or `sata-large` — never `ssd` or `ssd-large`; always set `storageClassName` explicitly
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-124-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-125-## Rust Build / Test Offloading
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-126-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:127:`cargo test` is intercepted by `~/.local/bin/cargo` on both this server and the lab. When inside a git repo with a remote and no uncommitted changes, it **automatically submits to iad-ci** via the `rust-verify` WorkflowTemplate (fmt + clippy + test), streams logs, and returns the exit code. If there are uncommitted changes or no remote, it falls back to a local cgroup-limited run (CPUQuota=200%, MemoryMax=6G).
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-128-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-129-All other cargo commands run locally under those same cgroup limits.
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-130-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-131-- `cargo-remote` is the submission script (`~/.local/bin/cargo-remote`)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-132-- The fallback is always safe — no test is silently skipped, it just runs locally with limits

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-290-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:291:- Read-only proxy runs in `devpod-observer` namespace with read-only RBAC — cannot create, delete, or modify resources (verified 2026-08-12: write and `get secrets` both return Forbidden); routed via Traefik `kubectl-tcp` entrypoint on the cluster's single Tailscale ingress. Manifests already exist at `declarative-config/k8s/iad-ci/devpod-observer/` and `traefik/tailscale-service.yml` — this was previously undocumented here, not previously unbuilt.
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:292:- ServiceAccount `argocd-manager` (admin kubeconfig) has **cluster-admin** — full read/write access; direct kubeconfig, not proxied
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:293:- CI/CD cluster running Argo Workflows for container builds; managed by rs-manager ArgoCD (`devpod-observer-ns-iad-ci`, `traefik-ns-iad-ci` apps)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:294:- A separate, narrowly-scoped `stuck-workflow-reaper` Deployment (also in `devpod-observer`) auto-deletes Argo Workflows whose pods are permanently stuck on `ImagePullBackOff`/`ErrImagePull` (900s grace period) — it can only `get`/`list` pods and `get`/`list`/`delete` workflows in `argo-workflows`, nothing else. This is the sanctioned pattern for "push a deletion": a narrowly-scoped, GitOps-deployed identity for one specific cleanup task, not broader write access through the observer proxy.
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:295:- For anything the reaper doesn't cover: all other writes go through `declarative-config/k8s/iad-ci/` → commit → push to Forgejo `main` → rs-manager ArgoCD syncs, same as every other cluster. There is no supported way to write through the read-only proxy itself — its RBAC is deliberately incapable of it.

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-473-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-474-```bash
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-475-# List recent workflow runs
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:476:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-479-# Get a workflow's phase and error message
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:480:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-481-  get workflow <name> -n argo-workflows \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-482-  -o jsonpath='{.status.phase} - {.status.message}'
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-483-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-484-# Get per-node failure details (use this when a step fails)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:485:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-486-  get workflow <name> -n argo-workflows -o json | python3 -c "
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-487-import json,sys
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-488-w = json.load(sys.stdin)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-489-for node in w['status'].get('nodes',{}).values():
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-490-    if node.get('phase') in ('Failed','Error'):
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-491-        print(node['displayName'], '-', node['phase'])
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-492-        print('  msg:', node.get('message',''))
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-493-"
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-494-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-495-# Stream logs from a running pod (must be caught WHILE running — podGC deletes on completion)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:496:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-497-  get pods -n argo-workflows -l workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=<name>
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-498-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:499:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-500-  logs -n argo-workflows <pod-name> -c main -f
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-501-```
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-502-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-503-> **Important:** The controller uses `podGC: OnPodCompletion` — pods are deleted the moment they finish. To capture logs from a failed step, either stream them while running, or submit a debug workflow with `podGC: OnWorkflowCompletion` override.

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-509-## Prerequisites
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-510-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:511:- **kubectl access:** `--kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig` (iad-ci cluster)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-512-- **jq installation:** `sudo apt install jq` (Debian/NixOS) or `brew install jq` (macOS)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-513-- **Target namespace:** `argo-workflows`
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-514-- **Date command compatibility:** The query supports both GNU date (`-d`) and BSD date (`-v`) via fallback syntax
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-515-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-516-## Complete Query (30-day window)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-517-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-518-```bash
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-519-# Get workflows from a specific template in the last 30 days
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-520-# Replace <template-name> with actual template (e.g., pbx-web-build, needle-ci)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:521:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-522-  get workflows -n argo-workflows \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-523-  -l workflows.argoproj.io/workflow-template=<template-name> \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-524-  -o json | jq \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-525-  --arg cutoff "$(date -u -d '30 days ago' +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ" 2>/dev/null || date -u -v-30d +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")" \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-526-  '{
--
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-596-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-597-**Workaround:** Query by workflow name pattern instead:
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-598-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-599-```bash
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-600-# Filter by workflow name prefix (more reliable)
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:601:kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-602-  get workflows -n argo-workflows -o json | jq \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-603-  --arg cutoff "$(date -u -d '30 days ago' +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ" 2>/dev/null || date -u -v-30d +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")" \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-604-  --arg prefix "pbx-web" \
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-605-  '{
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-606-    cutoff_date: $cutoff,

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-630-```

/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-632-## Troubleshooting
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-633-
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-634-**Empty results when workflows should exist:**
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md:635:1. Verify you're in the correct cluster: `kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig config current-context`
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-636-2. Check the namespace: `kubectl get workflows -n argo-workflows --no-headers | head -10`
/home/coding/CLAUDE.md-637-3. Verify label existence: `kubectl get workflows -n argo-workflows -o json | jq '.items[].metadata.labels | select(."workflows.argoproj.io/workflow-template")'`

codex
A preflight found the current `v0.1.3` tag points to an older bead-rs commit and is absent from the workflow’s depth-1 clone, so an unparameterized manual run would pass CI and then inevitably fail while trying to recreate that immutable tag. To produce the required Succeeded acceptance run without changing normal release behavior, I’m adding an opt-in `verification-only` workflow parameter (default `false`) that exits only after the full fmt/MSRV/stable/clippy/test sequence.
apply patch
patch: completed

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'workflow_name=bead-rs-ci-msrv-manual-kjf5z
for attempt in $(seq 1 60); do
  pod_name=$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get pods -l "workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=${workflow_name}" -o jsonpath='"'{.items[0].metadata.name}' 2>/dev/null)
  pod_phase="'$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get pods -l "workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=${workflow_name}" -o jsonpath='"'{.items[0].status.phase}' 2>/dev/null)
  if [ -n \""'$pod_name" ]; then
    printf '"'pod=%s phase=%s\\n' \""'$pod_name" "${pod_phase:-Pending}"
  fi
  if [ "$pod_phase" = "Running" ]; then
    kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows logs -f "$pod_name" -c main | rg --line-buffered '"'"'^'"\\+|MSRV|rustup|cargo|Finished|test result|CI passed|Verification-only|error:|failed'
    exit "'${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  fi
  workflow_phase=$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get workflow "$workflow_name" -o jsonpath='"'{.status.phase}' 2>/dev/null)
  case \""'$workflow_phase" in
    Failed|Error|Succeeded) printf '"'workflow reached %s before log streaming\\n' \""'$workflow_phase"; exit 1 ;;
  esac
  sleep 2
done
exit 2' in /home/coding/declarative-config

codex
The retry confirmed the blocker: the live pod could not anonymously clone the private Forgejo repository (`could not read Username`), before any Rust command ran. The template already receives `FORGEJO_TOKEN`; I’m wiring it through an ephemeral mode-600 `.netrc` (never in argv or logs), reusing that for the existing tag push, and cleaning it via an EXIT trap. This is required for the requested lane to execute at all.
apply patch

import yaml
path = Path('k8s/iad-ci/argo-workflows/bead-rs-ci-workflowtemplate.yml')
doc = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
script = doc['spec']['templates'][0]['container']['args'][0]
required = [
    'umask 077',
    '''printf 'machine git.ardenone.com\\\\nlogin x-token\\\\npassword %s\\\\n' \""'$FORGEJO_TOKEN" > "$HOME/.netrc"'"''',

]
for value in required:
    assert value in script, value
assert 'https://x-token:"'${FORGEJO_TOKEN}@'"' not in script
print('YAML parsed; Forgejo credential stays out of clone/push argv and netrc cleanup is installed')

  if printf '%s' \""'$live_script" | grep -q '"'machine git.ardenone.com'; then auth_state=present; fi
  printf '%s attempt=%s mirror=%s app=%s auth=%s\\n' \""'$(date -u +%H:%M:%S)" "$attempt" "$mirror" "$app" "$auth_state"
  if [ "$mirror" = "$target_revision" ] && [ "$app" = "$target_revision" ] && [ "$auth_state" = "present" ]; then exit 0; fi
  sleep 10
done
exit 2' in /home/coding/declarative-config

/bin/bash -lc 'workflow_name=bead-rs-ci-msrv-manual-4vjql
for attempt in $(seq 1 60); do
  pod_name=$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get pods -l "workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=${workflow_name}" -o jsonpath='"'{.items[0].metadata.name}' 2>/dev/null)
  pod_phase="'$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get pods -l "workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=${workflow_name}" -o jsonpath='"'{.items[0].status.phase}' 2>/dev/null)
  if [ -n \""'$pod_name" ]; then printf '"'pod=%s phase=%s\\n' \""'$pod_name" "${pod_phase:-Pending}"; fi
  if [ "$pod_phase" = "Running" ]; then
    kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows logs -f "$pod_name" -c main | rg --line-buffered '"'"'^'"\\+|MSRV|rustup|cargo|Finished|test result|CI passed|Verification-only|error:|fatal:|remote:'
    exit "'${PIPESTATUS[0]}
  fi
  workflow_phase=$(kubectl --kubeconfig=/home/coding/.kube/iad-ci.kubeconfig -n argo-workflows get workflow "$workflow_name" -o jsonpath='"'{.status.phase}' 2>/dev/null)
  case \""'$workflow_phase" in
    Failed|Error|Succeeded) printf '"'workflow reached %s before log streaming\\n' \""'$workflow_phase"; exit 1 ;;
  esac
  sleep 2
done
exit 2' in /home/coding/declarative-config

+            # Verify the project still declares the toolchain used by the
+            # pinned MSRV lane before compiling with it.
+            MSRV=1.85
+            DECLARED_MSRV=$(grep -m1 '^rust-version[[:space:]]*=' Cargo.toml | sed 's/.*= *"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/')
+            if [ "$DECLARED_MSRV" != "$MSRV" ]; then
+              echo "Cargo.toml rust-version '${DECLARED_MSRV}' does not match CI MSRV '${MSRV}'" >&2
+              exit 1
+            fi
+            rustup toolchain install "$MSRV" --profile minimal
+            cargo "+${MSRV}" check --all-targets
+
+            if [ "{{workflow.parameters.msrv-only}}" = "true" ]; then
+              echo "MSRV-only run passed; skipping stable and release steps"
+              exit 0
+            fi

             # CI checks
             cargo fmt --all -- --check
             cargo check --all-targets

    Failed|Error|Succeeded) printf '"'workflow reached %s before log streaming\\n' \""'$workflow_phase"; exit 1 ;;
  esac

  Without --json: Human-readable detailed view

  --comments none         Show only counts and resolution metadata (default)
  --comments unresolved   Include bodies for unresolved comments only
  --comments all          Include all comment bodies in canonical order

    93	            # Tag-to-Forgejo (draft-revert root-cause fix, 2026-08-16): the
    94	            # GitHub repo is a Forgejo push mirror, and every mirror sync
    95	            # PRUNES refs that exist only on GitHub. GitHub demotes a
    96	            # published release to draft when its tag is deleted, so the tag

   111	            # needle-workflowtemplate.yml.

codex
One final integrity check exposed a bead CLI projection issue: `bead close --reason` succeeded, but `bead show --json` renders both `close_reason` and `closed_at` as null. I’m checking the underlying issue event/history before deciding whether a safe re-close is necessary, because the workflow evidence must be durably recorded rather than merely passed on the command line.
exec
/bin/bash -lc "bead --help | sed -n '1,260p'
bead close --help | sed -n '1,200p'
bead update --help | sed -n '1,240p'" in /home/coding/bead-rs
 succeeded in 0ms:
bead-rs is an independent Rust task-coordination system.

The intended workflow is:
  init workspace -> create beads -> add blocking relationships
  -> inspect ready work -> claim -> update/release -> close -> flush checkpoint

The ready frontier can be inspected with `bead list --ready --json --limit N`,
which uses claim order but does not reserve the displayed beads. Use `bead claim`
to atomically assign work.

SQLite (.beads/beads.db) is the authoritative live state between flushes, and is
not committed. The checkpoint under .beads/checkpoint/ is the portable, durable
copy and is what Git tracks; flush it with `bead sync flush-only` before
committing the repository. Mutating commands never flush implicitly.

Lifecycle transitions:
  - open beads may be ready if unassigned and not manually blocked
  - unfinished `blocks` edges remove beads from the ready frontier
  - claim atomically assigns one ready bead and moves it to in_progress
  - release returns claimed work to open/unassigned
  - close requires a reason and may expose dependents
  - reopen restores a closed bead to open

EXIT CODES:
  0  success
  1  internal failure
  2  CLI usage or validation error
  3  workspace, issue, or file not found
  4  conflict (invalid transition, revision guard, cycle)
  5  malformed input or integrity failure

Run `bead <COMMAND> --help` for the full description of any command.

Usage: bead <COMMAND>

Commands:
  init          Initialize a new workspace
  create        Create a new issue
  list          List issues
  show          Show a single issue
  update        Update an issue
  release       Release a claimed issue
  close         Close an issue
  reopen        Reopen a closed issue
  claim         Claim an issue from the ready frontier
  label         Manage labels
  dep           Manage dependencies
  ref           Manage external references
  sync          Synchronize checkpoint operations
  doctor        Diagnose workspace integrity and optionally perform repairs
  capabilities  Show capabilities and supported features
  schema        Inspect public schemas
  query         Execute safe query language queries
  changes       Access cursor-based change feed for incremental local synchronization
  why           Explain issue state, readiness, blockers, and legal operations
  compare       Compare issue representation across two profiles
  data          Manage structured bead data
  recurrence    Manage recurrence templates
  policy        Validate workspace policy and scheduling configuration
  help          Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version
Close an issue with a required reason.

Close transitions an issue to closed status and records the close reason.
The reason must be non-empty and is preserved for audit and debugging.
Closing clears manual blocking and may expose dependent issues.

EXAMPLES:
  bead close bead-123abc456789def --reason "Completed successfully"
  bead close ID --reason "Fixed authentication bug"
  bead close ID --reason "Duplicate of ID-456"
  bead close ID --reason "Done" --if-revision 3    # With revision guard

SEMANTICS:
  - All non-closed states → closed (semantic close)
  - closed with matching reason → no-op, idempotent
  - closed with different reason → conflict (exit 4)

REQUIREMENTS:
  --reason TEXT must be non-empty after trimming whitespace

EFFECTS:
  - Sets base_status to closed
  - Clears manual_blocked flag
  - Sets closed_at to current time
  - Stores close_reason
  - Advances updated_at
  - Increments revision
  - Appends 'closed' audit event

REVISION GUARDS:
  --if-revision N provides optimistic concurrency control.
  If the issue's current revision doesn't match N, the close fails with
  exit code 4 and a conflict message. This prevents silent lost updates
  when multiple agents or humans modify the same issue concurrently.

IDEMPOTENCY:
  Repeating the same close command with the same reason is idempotent
  and does not change timestamps or append duplicate events.
  Changing the reason is a conflict (use update with new reason if needed).

Dependent issues may become ready when their last blocker is closed.

Usage: bead close [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>
          Issue ID

Options:
      --reason <REASON>
          Close reason (required)

      --if-revision <IF_REVISION>
          Expected revision for optimistic concurrency control

      --fencing-token <FENCING_TOKEN>
          Fencing token for lease validation (advanced usage)

      --dry-run
          Dry run: show what would happen without making changes

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version
Update issue fields atomically.

Updates one or more issue fields in a single atomic transaction.
All validations and mutations occur together; any failure leaves the
issue unchanged. Changing status to 'closed' requires the 'close' command
instead. Use 'reopen' to transition from closed to open.

EXAMPLES:
  bead update ID --status in_progress                # Start working on an issue
  bead update ID --assignee alice                    # Assign to alice
  bead update ID --notes "Investigated root cause"   # Add investigation notes
  bead update ID --clear-assignee                    # Clear assignment (open only)
  bead update ID --status in_progress --if-revision 3 # With revision guard

STATUS TRANSITIONS:
  Valid transitions depend on the current base status:
  - open can transition to: in_progress, deferred
  - in_progress can transition to: open, deferred, closed (via 'close' command)
  - deferred can transition to: open, closed (via 'close' command)
  - closed can only transition to: open (via 'reopen' command)

FIELDS THIS COMMAND DOES NOT CHANGE:
  Title, description, priority, issue type, and labels are not editable here.
  Set them at creation time with 'bead create', and use 'bead label add' or
  'bead label remove' for labels. Passing --priority or --title is a usage error.

ASSIGNMENT:
  --assignee and --clear-assignee are mutually exclusive.
  --clear-assignee only works for open assigned issues.
  For in_progress issues, use 'bead release' to return to open/unassigned.

BLOCKED STATUS:
  Setting --status blocked sets manual_blocked=true and retains base status.
  Setting --status open clears manual blocking and sets base status to open.
  Closed issues cannot use --status open (use 'reopen' instead).

REVISION GUARDS:
  --if-revision N provides optimistic concurrency control.
  If the issue's current revision doesn't match N, the update fails with
  exit code 4 and a conflict message. This prevents silent lost updates
  when multiple agents or humans modify the same issue concurrently.

All updates are atomic. Invalid transitions or conflicts exit with code 4
without changing any fields or timestamps.

Usage: bead update [OPTIONS] <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>
          Issue ID

Options:
      --status <STATUS>
          New status

      --assignee <ASSIGNEE>
          New assignee

      --clear-assignee
          Clear assignee (only for open assigned issues)

      --notes <NOTES>
          New notes

      --if-revision <IF_REVISION>
          Expected revision for optimistic concurrency control

      --fencing-token <FENCING_TOKEN>
          Fencing token for lease validation (advanced usage)

      --dry-run
          Dry run: show what would happen without making changes

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

CREATE TABLE issues (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    description TEXT DEFAULT '',
    notes TEXT DEFAULT '',
    priority INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (priority >= 0 AND priority <= 4),
    issue_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'task',
    base_status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (base_status IN ('open', 'in_progress', 'deferred', 'closed')),
    manual_blocked INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (manual_blocked IN (0, 1)),
    assignee TEXT,
    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    updated_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    closed_at TEXT,
    close_reason TEXT,
    source_repo TEXT,
    profile TEXT DEFAULT 'native-v1',
    schema_ref TEXT DEFAULT 'urn:bead-rs:schema:issue:native-v1'
, revision INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, ready_since TEXT, last_claim_sequence INTEGER, attempt_tier INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (attempt_tier >= 0 AND attempt_tier <= 3), consecutive_failures INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, retry_after_claim_sequence INTEGER);
CREATE INDEX issues_readiness ON issues (
    base_status, manual_blocked, assignee, priority, created_at, id
);
CREATE INDEX issues_revision ON issues (id, revision);
CREATE INDEX issues_ready_frontier ON issues (base_status, manual_blocked, assignee, priority, created_at, id);
CREATE INDEX issues_attempt_tier ON issues (attempt_tier, last_claim_sequence);
